In this respect, the new rector of Tallinn University, my alma mater, Tõnu Viik, says that culture is the way of life of the great communities, the world view, the organisation of life and society, and all their achievements in spirit. The preamble to the Estonian Constitution also mentions culture in this sense. Tõnu Viik also says that this is not about the size of the people, but about their ability to develop their own culture and language. The R&D&I draft rightly states that humanities research is very important for the development of the Estonian state, society, nationality and language, including the study of the operational models of Estonian society, solving the development needs of society and supporting adaptation to global trends. It is important to develop this area even if it lacks direct economic value and potential. But in this case, how is it possible that in an Estonian country where the preservation of the mother tongue and culture is considered to be a constitutional value, the funding of humanities is significantly lower than in other disciplines? I will leave this rhetorical question in the air here. TAIE says: Activities in the field of a vibrant Estonian society, language and cultural space are a prerequisite and a basis for achieving the goals of other areas of focus. Implementation, however, is not just about supporting studies of Estonian language, Estonian history and culture or developing language technology. For example, smart specialisation, circular economy, tourism as part of an experience economy are related to humanities. Our success story of e-government and e-governance is certainly linked to the heritage of Estonian spiritual culture: beliefs, attitudes, values, work practices and so on. In the context of entrepreneurship and innovation, I would like to mention the creative industries as a separate issue. I gave a long talk on this subject during the thematic debate on the European Union's instruments in this Chamber, but I now recall that the promotion of creative industries has been based on the growth strategy of Estonian business in the last seven years, which has just ended its validity and where sectoral challenges and activities were described in the chapter on the competitive position. When reading the R&D&I draft, one can only assume and assume where the promotion of creative industries could fit. The question arises as to why the cultural and creative sectors are not considered as an economic sector within the framework of TAIE, which deserves intellectual property, creates prosperity and jobs, and provides creativity as a competence to address societal challenges. Creative economy is part of a smart and knowledge-based economy; the cultural and creative sectors are part of a thrill economy, increasing Estonia's reputation and contributing to the development of tourism and regional competitiveness. The issue of the development of the creative economy has been mentioned only on the subject sheet, which is an additional material for the development plan. Finally, the most important issue: the situation in higher education and the sneaking-up of paid higher education, thereby reducing access to higher education. Ladies and gentlemen, If the situation with the funding of higher education does not improve as a matter of urgency by contributing to higher education, we will have no research, development, innovation or entrepreneurship in a few years' time. We have already come so far that even the rectors of universities are willing to introduce even paid higher education, but sustainability can be ensured. According to a joint education agreement recently concluded by the largest organisations in the education and research sector in Estonia, supported by social democrats, state funding for higher education should be increased to at least one-and-a-half percent of GDP. At least the same level of funding needs to be ensured. I very much hope that the other parties will join this agreement and that it will become a new social agreement. There is no other option, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you for your attention and I wish all of us good science and good innovation.

Nor is there a fixed need to make the criteria for evaluating and financing research projects more Estonia-centred. It is precisely these criteria, which may seem like small things, that are actually the key to the emphasis of Estonian research. Secondly, it is certainly appropriate to reduce the project orientation leading to the hyphenity of research, but there is still no indication of how to keep science competitive, to avoid stagnation and friendship of research groups and institutions, which is undoubtedly taking place in Estonia and maybe, as a result of the decline in project orientation, becomes an even bigger problem. Thirdly, an appropriate reference has been made to the insufficient follow-up of the scientific community and to the need to increase the efficiency and attractiveness of doctoral studies, but no attention has been paid to the problem of devaluation of doctoral level and quality of higher education at all. The doctorate is not about itself; it is about fixing one stage, in which letting the bar down does not in any way improve Estonian science or society. Fourthly, it is appropriate to draw attention to the small capacity of companies located in Estonia to create new business models and adapt and introduce new knowledge and technologies. The reason has been to attract foreign capital without choice, which results mainly in the state of assembly plants owned by foreign owners and of low-paid citizens. The solution is to support the establishment of domestic plants, but this has not been given sufficient attention in the development plan. Fifthly, the emphasis on supporting businesses or supporting them from export intensity to knowledge intensity and innovation is appropriate. At the same time, start-ups have been overestimated for no good reason and too much attention has been paid to them. In fact, the focus should be on all start-ups and attention also needs mature and traditional entrepreneurship. As a matter of fact, I must say that we must support what has proved its viability. Sixthly, the text has become more meaningful in the process, but there is still too much slogan, clichés and bureaucratic pulpit that does not really build trust in rational scientists and entrepreneurs. Seven. It is a good thing that the development plan has been sought to translate into targeted objectives, but some of the metrics are mechanical, not to say unnecessary. For example, 10% of the most quoted scientific articles in the world should include 12.5% of Estonian articles. Well, this kind of bibliometry has at least been ridiculing the humanitarians for decades. And at least in the humanities and social sciences, it has become very damaging, because scientists are rationally thinking people, and this basis of assessment also directs us to publish articles in English-language journals beyond the corner. Finally, there are targets, which may also be characteristic. If the distribution of Estonian research money is not more closely linked to the needs of Estonia's business and Estonian society, the current, predominantly foreign-owned assembly industry cannot ensure the interest of entrepreneurship in research development and innovation of the economy, nor can it provide enough added value. Under similar conditions as before, no other result can be obtained. Elementary! You don't have to be an experimental scientist to know that. In this case, the increase in private R &amp; D expenditure from 0.59% to 2% of GDP is also not realistic as a target. It's not real. The total increase in labour productivity is 110% compared to the EU average. Right now we have 74%. The increase in the number of scientists and engineers is also threefold unachievable. Thank you.

Microbiologists wondered why a lactobacile is such a good fighter against harmful pathogenic microbes. The basic studies helped to clarify the mechanism of action of lactic acid bacteria. The antioxidant ability of Lactobacillus fermentum ME-3 enables it to protect the body's cells against stress. It was only when this discovery was protected in scientific articles that applied research, patent application and product development followed. The University of Tartu has other proposals in store: gas fermentation technologies for processing municipal waste, personal medicine applications for public health, hydrogen technologies for energy storage, carbon sequestration applications in agriculture, artificial intelligence and the use of machine learning in different economic sectors. ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U54	[[Remote connection]] Good members of the Riigikogu, good night, everyone! For the start, thank you very much for the debate on the substance of the previous item. Indeed, the previous development plan that you dealt with was born in cooperation with the two ministries, but the two of us could not speak to the Riigikogu about it. This time, we deliberately chose the Minister of Enterprise as rapporteur for the development plan to show that this area is really extremely important to both parties, and the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications contributed a great deal to this. Today, however, I will introduce you to the Youth Development Plan until 2035. Developments in the field of youth have been planned for 20 years at the level of the Government of the Republic. The first Youth Work Agenda was completed in 2001 and this follow-up strategy is already the fourth in line. During this time, Estonia's youth sector has achieved an exemplary position in Europe and in the world. The OECD has repeatedly highlighted the depth and integrity of Estonian youth policy. In 2019, we received the Golden Prize for Future Policy of the World Council for the so far strategic planning of the youth field. Estonia has been recognised for ensuring the broad participation of young people, the creation of opportunities for participation and the diversity of self-realization opportunities. What makes our youth field so special? In Estonia's approach, youth work and youth policy are included in the youth field. In other words, the creation and provision of broad opportunities and the background system affecting young people are linked. There are countries where youth work focuses mainly on dealing with young people with fewer opportunities. The key to Estonia's success is to ensure that all young people in general, regardless of their background or status, have access to diverse opportunities and support by applying different forms of youth work. The impact of the COVID-19 pandemic has undoubtedly exacerbated the challenges facing young people in terms of education, employment, income and also mental health. We know that it is young people who have been hit hardest in the labour market. During the first wave, the number of 16-24-year-olds unemployed increased by a third. Based on surveillance studies conducted during the pandemic, the highest rates of depression are also seen in the same age group. However, the impact on young people of learning gaps caused by distance learning may not be felt until years later. The OECD reports published last year highlighted the need to create and modernise national youth strategies in the context of a pandemic. Planning the development of the youth field as a whole will help to avoid fragmentation, manage better and reduce the consequences of the pandemic. The youth sector covers different ministerial responsibilities, as well as the activities of local authorities and private service providers. The importance of maintaining a comprehensive picture has been stressed by our partner ministries during the consultations. Similar recommendations have been made by the European Union, the UN and the Council of Europe. As of 2019, there were nearly 276000 young people in Estonia (from 7 to 26 years of age), representing 21% of the total population. This is the lowest in recent history, and according to Statistics Estonia's forecasts it will remain at the same level until at least 2035. In the last ten years alone, the number of young people has fallen by 17%. Young people are undoubtedly the key to the survival of our country, their creativity and their civic contribution will help to move society forward, which is why attention must be paid to them now and now. Young people's participation in youth work activities has increased year after year. While 37% of the population aged 7 to 26 were involved in the various activities of youth work in 2010, this share is now approaching 60%. Young people's workforce has also grown in one of the youth work services. The number of specialists working with young people has increased by over 9,000. It is a pleasure to note that young people appreciate the opportunities offered. Based on a survey of young people's satisfaction at the beginning of April, 90% of those involved in youth work are satisfied or very satisfied with what they have experienced. The greatest source of satisfaction is the social environment offered by youth work, relations with mentors and peers. Youth workers have an excellent ability to detect early challenges faced by young people by listening to them, providing advice and support, taking into account the specific expectations and needs of young people. Unfortunately, not all young people feel socially involved. According to the study "Children's subjective well-being at school," in recent years 24 to 35% of young people have experienced bullying at school. According to the International Children's Welfare Survey, 36% of respondents believe that adults do not listen or take them seriously enough. Up to 42% of respondents assessed the possibilities to participate in decisions concerning them as limited. The sense of belonging and the perception of inclusion depend on whether young people's thoughts are heard and on the considerable response at home, at school and in the community. Also, whether young people feel connected with their peers, whether their joint activities are meaningful in the community. Of course, we must not forget young people who are at risk of being excluded from special health needs. It is important to view young people as we want to see young people view their community and society as a whole. It should be borne in mind that children and young people have significantly more limited rights and opportunities, for example when entering the labour market or accessing economic resources, compared to other age groups. That is why young people deserve special attention. Although a large proportion of our young people are active in education or the labour market, the COVID-19 pandemic has led to a significant increase in the proportion of NEETs. These are young people who do not learn or work. At present, 1 in 10 young people are in this situation. Young people experience major transitions, changes, and obstacles through independence and social maturity. In order for these experiences to be meaningful and meaningful and to increase their readiness to cope with changes in the future, young people may need additional support, for example by allowing flexible opportunities for young people to develop diverse learning pathways in cooperation with education. Young people need knowledge and skills to cope with change, which in turn is the basis for attitudes. In view of the trends of the future and the complex challenges we face as a society, it is important to create opportunities for, among other things, digital competences, critical analysis and problem-solving, as well as social and emotional skills. Alongside the education system, it is the youth sector that plays an important role. The draft Youth Development Plan is the result of a long and comprehensive process of engagement. In autumn 2018, the first inclusion forums for young people and youth practitioners were organised, with the active involvement of local authorities and stakeholders. More than 60 organisations were represented in the 2019 working groups. The basic principles formulated in the co-creation process and the challenges described have become the backbone of the objectives and activities of the development plan. According to the vision set out in the development plan, this is a very ambitious young person living in 2035 in all regions of Estonia and is able to change the community and the country in such a way that Estonia has the best environment for growth, living and self-realization. We aim to provide young people with broad opportunities for development, security and solid support, thereby creating Estonia, which the young person wants to take forward. In order to achieve the vision, the development plan sets out four strategic objectives: firstly, young people are creative and a driving force for society; secondly, the protection of young people's rights in the country is consistent and the active participation of young people is supported; thirdly, high-quality youth work creates opportunities for young people to develop in a variety of ways, to experience success, to enrich their experiences and to become independent; and fourthly, young people's loneliness and withdrawal will be noticed and prevented by means of a safety net that enhances confidence. What are the changes that await a new period of time? Young people have great potential to be the initiators and leaders of social change. The development of young people's creativity and entrepreneurship requires support for young people's self-confidence and self-confidence, enabling them to experience mistakes and successes and learn from choices. Young people's talents, interests and creativity must be consistently recognised, including through cooperation with education and employers, taking better account of the knowledge, skills and experience acquired in non-formal learning. We have entrusted young people with the right to vote in municipal elections. In 72 municipalities all over Estonia there is a youth participation council or active group next to the council. In addition, it is estimated that 10% of young people participate in the activities of youth associations. The contribution of young people as volunteers is no less important. Up to 69% of young people aged 15 to 24 have contributed to volunteering, accounting for one fifth of all volunteers. A better understanding of young people's expectations and needs is essential, especially for those who are not currently taking part in or aware of the opportunities of youth work. In the youth field, the development of tools to measure the quality of activities and services for young people has been systematically addressed, for example through a tool for evaluating the quality of youth work for local authorities and the development of data analysis and monitoring capabilities in the field. In order to ensure that no young person misses or misses the necessary support, it is necessary to keep and develop knowledge of the current situation of our young people and to ensure up-to-date cooperation with specialists in the fields of education, social protection, child protection and internal security. Of course, it is based on motivated and supported youth workers who are guaranteed appropriate training opportunities and social guarantees. I would like to thank all young people, youth workers, representatives of municipalities and other stakeholders who have contributed and given valuable ideas to the preparation of the development plan. This development plan is the voice of young people themselves, describing the future that young people really want for themselves. I believe that by 2035 Estonia will indeed be the best environment for youth growth and development in the world, with young people and moving towards the goals and goals set for young people. Thank you.

It is exciting to discuss whether this is a task for the state or the municipality, but it is actually a challenge for the whole society. Our youth's so-called better role has been taken up in the field of recreational education. These are young people who are already active, are engaged in various hobbies and are learning exemplaryly. However, young people who have not found a place in our society may be excluded from the spectrum of recreational education and activities. Their involvement is an even greater challenge. What message does such an approach provide for young people? Are they welcome to the so-called adult world? Although statistics and forecasts show that people's lives are increasing, we have no cause for complacency. In addition to life expectancy, healthy years are also important, where we have room for improvement, both for young people and, in fact, for adults. Risk behaviour is still a very topical issue and, unfortunately, the mortality rates of young people are still high due to risk behaviour. In addition to the eternal problems, our young people face new challenges, such as digital threats, cyber fraud and cyber bullying. All of this affects young people's mental health and promotes excessive self - criticism, stress, and unhealthy habits. The development plan for the youth field has been launched precisely to ensure broad development opportunities, security and solid support for Estonian young people. Risk behaviour is not the only concern. Youth unemployment, youth migration, young people's participation in national and local issues must contribute to all these areas if we want to see a brighter future. In order to respond to these challenges, the following actions have been identified in the development plan. Supporting young people in independence and transition to work life, ensuring opportunities to engage in representative democracy, improving access to youth work activities for groups to which services do not reach today, raising awareness of the need for equal treatment for young people, creating opportunities for self-detection, broadening the horizon, self-initiative and collective actions, increasing access to youth work activities and institutions, ensuring the systematic development and availability of youth information quality services, and enriching forms of youth work, the mobility of services and dynamism are most suited to the needs of young people. It is important to note that in the case of youth work, the state will only create a legal framework and support the municipality on strategic priorities and ensuring a consistent quality of services. The main level of organisation of youth work is the local government. Their role is to ensure the development and provision of youth services to young people. It depends on local governments how good a network is to support the development of young people. The choice and priorities of municipalities depend heavily on whether or not at least one circle of interest is guaranteed for each young person and how attractive local youth centres are. Finally, I would like to thank the former Minister for Education and today the Chairman of the Estonian Centre Party Group, Mailis Reps, who led the document that was so important for our future. But, of course, I would also like to thank Mr Aab and Mrs Kersna, who have completed this process. The development plan is the basis for promoting the lives of young people throughout Estonia. This is a basic document for the future of Estonia. Thank you for your attention.

As Minister for Finance, I can honestly provide information and an overview of the country's real financial capacity, the tax revenue that taxpayers bring to the budget and within what limits. In each specific area, however, these choices can only be made by the head of the field, who is a minister in each specific area of government. That is the essence of the policy. Why else would this minister be needed if he cannot or does not have to make these substantive choices? As far as recreational education is concerned, municipalities actually report a series of ways in which there will be no problem with the funding of recreational education. This has been done by small municipalities, we call Järva parish, but it has also been done by large cities, the last one can be called Tallinn. That's natural. Financing of recreational education is by law the responsibility of municipalities. I am grateful to all the municipalities who understand the solidarity with the country during this very difficult financial situation. If the budget revenues of the municipalities rise, as they will in the coming years, the local governments will be able to continue with both the groups of interest and the education of interest, without causing any changes to the disadvantage. Very many municipalities have promised to do so. Once again, thank you and congratulate them. The intermediate hysteria that accompanied the 7 million changes was actually redundant. The reason for this, too, must be sought out in the approaching local elections and the heightened need for attention. But when it comes to alcohol excise, let's say the numbers. Perhaps, in the case of tests, I think it is right to hold this debate with health experts, rather than decide in any way at political level what is right or wrong in the number of reproductions and what the sensitivity of the correct test should be. These tests are deliberately equivalent to the so-called gold standard worldwide; they are very sensitive to detect these positive particles, or coronavirus RNA, in the human body. Often, it can be said that [human] may already be residual positive, that it has suffered from this disease, but this sensitivity helps to find positive results more quickly and, if necessary, to direct or assist people in self - isolation. Perhaps even if there are people among them who are residually positive or have passed through, it is also an important finding or knowledge in comparison to the fact that these tests are significantly less sensitive and sick people would not be identified and spread this infection. Looking at the decline in our infection rates, it can be seen that the results of these same tests, the same methodologies, show very clearly that vaccination, restrictions and, to say the same thing, seasonality have a clear effect. We see that the proportion of positive tests is decreasing, we see that the number of positive tests is decreasing. All of this is correlated with the need for hospital care, the picture we see in the view of deaths. In the principle, we can see that these trends support and confirm each other. There is no situation where hospital care, for example, would fall, but at the same time the proportion of positive tests would increase or vice versa, but rather they show that the methodology of the tests, the testing options chosen, have been correct. But I suggest you really send these questions in writing, you will be able to answer in writing about various tests, different methodologies, different forms of laboratory analysis that are taken into account in the daily Health Board review of positive tests.

According to the report, there are fur farms where the needs of wild animals cannot be met. Also, the best animal welfare tests cannot raise animal welfare to what most people consider sufficient. ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U321	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Good Riigikogu! This bill is bad, both in substance and form. First of all, the contents. It is clear that there is actually no substantive problem with fur farmers in Estonia. The decision is symbolic and the signs have come from Europe. If we look at what has happened in countries that have reached similar decisions, then the next step is to ban chicken, pig and sheep farming. Very dangerous tendency! And what to deny there was also the request of the animalguards who participated in the committee meeting. Good ambassadors! Should this draft become a law, there will certainly be new disputes about the possibility of keeping the next species of animals and birds. Coming to the bill now, the definition of fur has been used in Estonian legislation a few times, but this definition has not been given. But you can imagine what they're talking about. Secondly, it uses two words with slightly different meanings: 'only' and'mainly'. I quote the text of the bill: it is forbidden to keep animals solely or mainly for the purpose of producing fur. The terms "only" and "mainly" used in the draft act are words of a common language which have no specific meaning given. They must therefore be interpreted by means of the meaning used in the common language. "One" means singularity, only one purpose. If the sole purpose of animal production development and reproduction is to produce fur, this activity would be prohibited under the draft. If the draft were to stop there, everything would be relatively clear. The meaning field of the word "mainly" is somewhat vague, but it is commonly understood as "more than half": more than half of cases, more than half of a quantity and so on. Consequently, in the case of the rearing and breeding of fur animals, some borderline cases may be constructed, where the answer to the question, which is the main purpose of the rearing, is not obvious. It is clear from the explanatory memorandum that the authors of the draft have wanted to exclude rabbits and sheep, for example, from the scope of the provision on keeping rabbits and sheep. For these two species, it is probably the hardest to estimate, which is the main purpose of their rearing, either meat or skin and wool. There is, in fact, an additional problem in providing this explanation. Bear rearing has been given a much broader meaning than we normally understand. Thus, in the context of this law, fur production must not only be interpreted in the cage-keeping sector, but also extends to livestock. Very slippery tea! We can also imagine a situation where someone has domesticated a fox cub and it basically lives as a pet. If, at the end of the animal's days, a person decides that he should still be able to collar the skin, the primary goal should probably be to keep a pet rather than to raise a fur animal. But if 20 foxes are found in someone's home, how do we interpret it? After all, there are many examples where, for example, 20 cats or dogs are kept in homes, why not also foxes as pets. As a result, the entry into force of this law may lead to litigation, starting with the fact that fox growers may need to analyse their goals and the size of the herd in a little more depth. However, nutria and chinchilla growers may be carefree under this draft because the meat of these animals is edible and by their nature they are treated in the same way as the domestic rabbit that the initiators of the draft wish to exclude. I really hope this bill doesn't become a law. Thank you.

That's the argument that you see, we start with this, but in the end, God knows what ugly thing happens. It is used very often and there are all kinds of variations in this type of argument. It's a demagogue thing, and it's one of the most classic. We have heard here that all right, we can ban these fur farms, but then we all have to become vegans, because the next thing we know is to ban all farm animals. No, that argument doesn't hurt. The first is that this is a problem called a problem of common operation in philosophy or logic: common action problem. Mr Ernits referred here to climate neutrality and, of course, we want everyone to do something for it, that is the aim. But if everyone says we're not gonna do anything because we're not sure everyone's gonna do anything, nobody's gonna do anything. And there will be no positive development. But we gotta start somewhere, the others will come for us. In fact, we have seen it both on fur farms and in this same move towards climate neutrality. With its ambitious climate targets, Europe has also succeeded in catching up with other countries. That's the first half of the answer. The other side of this answer: well, let's take the analogy. In Estonia, we may reduce logging, but then more forests will be cut down in Belarus. True, this may happen, but the Estonian forest will remain. I showed one picture last time: to get a kilogram of some skin, you need 11.4 mints, and to grow 11.4 mints, you need to feed 563 kg of food. That's where these massive CO2 emissions come from. In addition, the chemicals used to process the skin and, of course, the nitrogen and phosphorus pollution that pollutes our land and water. It's all part of it. So, yes, indeed, it would be nice to agree on a large number of environmental aspects globally. That'd be great! But it is not possible to do so; we can make these decisions ourselves. The argument that this is an industry is an industry. But there are other bad sectors of the economy, such as methamphetamine has a very large market, a global one, but that does not mean that we believe that this should be legalised. We don't think so. ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U341	Let us move on to today's second item, the first reading of the draft law amending the Law on excise duties on alcohol, tobacco, fuel and electricity initiated by the Isamaa Group. The presentation is made by Aivar Kokk, member of the Riigikogu. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U342	[[Remote connection]] Good manager! Ladies and gentlemen, The draft amends the Law on excise duties on alcohol, tobacco, fuel and electricity with a view to preventing the excise duty increase that will take place in April next year and to mitigating the economic impact on businesses and private consumers. The excise duty on diesel from EUR 493 to EUR 372 per 1000 litres increased the sales of diesel in Estonia and reduced border trade. In addition, it reduced the costs for businesses, which helped them to better adapt to the crisis situation. Eight months after the reduction of excise duty, Estonia sold about 11% more diesel fuel than a year earlier. The transport sector has been talking about the positive effects of reducing the excise duty on diesel. They have pointed out that the fall in excise duty that came into force last year improved their position in international competition. According to 2019 data, there were only five Member States in the European Union where the excise duty on diesel was higher than in Estonia. These were Italy, Belgium, France, the Netherlands and Ireland. However, only in the Netherlands there was no refund of excise duty on lorries. Perhaps Estonia was one of the largest excise duties imposed on transport companies in Europe. In the case of increases or reductions in excise duty rates, each government shall be accompanied by the same dispute. On the one hand, excise duties on diesel and electricity bring significant revenue to the state budget. On the other hand, too high excise rates encourage border trade and increase the share of the secret market and undermine the livelihood of our people. After the world began to be shaken by the unprecedented COVID-19 pandemic, we decided that concrete steps are needed to improve the livelihood of Estonian people and the competitive conditions of companies. That is why we have reduced 11 excise duties from natural gas and fuel oils to diesel and electricity. What is the result of this decision? In the case of diesel, this is a success story. Estonian companies brought their refueling from neighbouring countries back to Estonia. The number of diesel tanks is 24% higher than at previous rates. The Ministry of Finance's spring economic forecast also shows that trade in diesel at the border, which reached 65 million litres last year, is expected to fall to 1.2% or 10 million litres this year. In difficult economic circumstances, the decision has clearly justified itself. It has also favoured the competitiveness of domestic companies. [[The connection's breaking up.]] ... before the fall in excise duty, it was not possible to compete with neighbouring countries even in domestic transport. Data from Statistics Estonia testify to this. During the period from 2015 to 2019, the cargo turnover of Estonian companies decreased by 23%. After the fall in excise duty, the trend has slowed down and the cargo turnover in the third quarter of last year was 44% higher than in the first quarter of last year. The Estonian Oil Association, which brings together Estonian fuel sector companies, has made it clear that the government does not have full information about the actual positive effects of the current excise duty on our state budget in planning the state budget. They have stated in their public address to the government and the Riigikogu that the spring economic forecast of the Ministry of Finance contains incorrect estimates of the impact of excise duties on domestic consumption volumes of diesel, and therefore the positive impact of the low level of excise duties on the receipts of the state budget does not come out of the document. The Oil Association is of the opinion that keeping excise duties at the same level increases the annual quantities of diesel fuel in Estonia by at least 110 to 120 million litres, twice as much as the Ministry of Finance has assessed. In addition, the two scenarios of raising excise duty to EUR 493 per 1000 litres and maintaining the current level of excise duty, as compared to the current level, have shown that the effect of keeping excise duty at the current level, even in the minimum scenario, will have a neutral effect on the revenue from the state budget compared to the increase in excise duty. The Estonian Oil Association has calculated that if excise duty rises back to 493 euros per 1000 litres, the price of diesel at petrol stations could be more than 1.5 euros a litre in the next winter, together with a possible increase in oil prices. In addition to the higher excise duty, Estonia will be more stringent than Latvia and Lithuania... due to the lack of... price difference at filling stations... from November next year... [[The connection's breaking up.]] For Estonian carriers, this fuel, which accounts for more than 50% of the cost of carriers, is more expensive than in neighbouring carriers. We have seen this before in the case of alcohol duty, when Estonian taxpayers supported the shops on the Latvian border with their money. The sharp rise in excise duty is nothing more than a proliferation of border trade. The Estonian Chamber of Agriculture and Trade has pointed out that, in view of the general competitive situation, it considers the amendment of the law initiated by the Isamaa Group to prevent the excise increases planned for the following year to be appropriate. According to the Chamber of Agriculture, the agricultural fuel excise duty should be reduced to a level comparable to that of Latvia and Lithuania. It is still unclear in what extent and whether the government plans to pay domestic subsidies next year. Depending on this decision, the increase in excise duty... may become even more important. The Estonian Food Industry Association has also referred to the significantly higher price of diesel compared to most other European countries, especially Latvia and Lithuania. This has a direct impact on logistics charges and transport prices. In particular, the large-scale indirect tax increases in the form of gas, electricity and fuel excise duties in previous years have been reflected in final prices for heavy duty and road use and have led to higher food prices for consumers. Estonia has been the leader of the rise in food and drink prices in the European Union, which affects Estonian consumers and concerns food processors. The latest consumer surveys clearly point to consumer price sensitivity and this is of concern. The Estonian Gas Association also supports the extension of lower excise rates initiated by Isamaa until 30 April 2023. This is also because Latvia and Lithuania have harmonised gas excise rates and are nearly 14 times cheaper than Estonia. The Polish excise duty rate is seven times cheaper and we have set an excise duty rate of 1.8 times higher than the Federal Republic of Germany. According to data from the European Commission's Directorate for Energy, Latvia's electricity excise duty is 1.01 euros for business customers, 0.52 euros per megawatt-hour in Lithuania, then in Estonia it is 4.47 euros. It is important to monitor the excise rates and differences set by neighbouring countries in the context of tax policy, especially in the business segment, as this is essential for competitiveness. One more point I want to bring in is the fight against climate change. In reality, the increase in excise duty on diesel has the opposite effect. As there are no solutions currently planned for heavy duty vehicles alongside the old diesel-using internal combustion engine, an increase in excise duty would not lead to a shift to new technologies. For heavy goods vehicles, the only way to reduce the climate impact of transport is to use liquid biofuels. By 2022, the share of biofuels in Estonian diesel will be 10%, i.e. 10% of fuel will have no CO2 emissions. The increase in excise duty would lead to the service of Estonian transport to Latvian, Lithuanian, Polish and other carriers who consume diesel with a lower biofuel content. For example, if an Estonian carrier were replaced by a Latvian carrier, the climate impact of a particular transport operation would increase by 10%. What's the solution? We have been in a crisis for more than a year now. Therefore, Isamaa has proposed a compromise to extend the reduced excise duty rates by one year in order to give companies additional time to recover under the economic crisis. At a time when the economy is still recovering, there is no additional tax burden on businesses. There is a need to look for ways to lower taxes and, if this fails, at least to prevent tax increases. These are simple principles and should be understood by every government. In order to prevent border trade, the excise policy of neighbours, in particular Latvia and Lithuania, needs to be monitored. Our excise duties should not be higher in the future than they do. When we cut excise duty on a fixed-term basis last year, we hoped that the crisis would not last long. During the discussions, we promised to come back to this issue during this year's discussions on the country's budgetary strategy and, if the crisis is going on, which is going on, and the neighbours have not increased the excise duty, to extend the current excise duty. Our immediate neighbours have not increased the excise duty, and therefore we should also prevent the rise in excise duty next year, without repeating the mistakes caused by border trade. The Estonian state should favour the competitiveness of our companies and mitigate the effects of the COVID-19 crisis. It is therefore necessary to extend the excise duty reduction until 1 May 2020. We keep our people and support Estonian entrepreneurs to have a level playing field with companies from other Baltic countries. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U379	Dear Deputy Speaker! Ladies and gentlemen, This draft is certainly a step in the right direction. In Estonia, diesel excise duties are currently reasonably low. But I would remind you that a little more than a year ago, in the previous government, not only diesel excise duty, but also gas excise duty and electricity excise duty were lowered, while electricity excise duty was reduced to the lowest possible level permitted by the European directives. The excise duty on diesel will undoubtedly have the greatest impact on the state budget, which will be collected more than other excise duties on fuel. However, the level of excise duties on gas is also very important on a sector-by-sector basis. Estonia had a crazyly high gas excise duty before it fell compared to the countries around us, and this excise duty has an impact on agriculture, the furniture industry and the chemical industry. The electric excise duty, however, affects all Estonian people. For the sake of the truth, one thing must be overcome. Unlike Aivar Coke, I was at the heart of the discussions at the time, with all those discussions on how much to reduce and whether the fall is temporary or permanent. Remember, the previous government also lowered excise duties on alcohol and this was done on a permanent basis. And the result of this reduction in alcohol duty is a significant reduction in border trade to a'reasonable' level, without the state budget having suffered any damage. True, we find it difficult to estimate statistics, for it is impossible to take such a comparison between the corona and the full years. But in the case of the reduction of diesel excise, it is a fact that it was a fraction of the Fatherland that categorically stood for it to be not a permanent downfall, but a temporary downfall. We agreed. The second option was that there would have been no fall at all. We agreed with this in the certainty that once we have done the fall, we will see its benefits, and that the benefits are so clear and so understandable that there are not such crazy people in Estonia who will allow the excise rise that we had then introduced into the law. In fact, in the previous government coalition, there was still a fundamental understanding that in a year's time, the effects of this fall would be re-examined and the rate would be moved to a permanent rate, i.e. the current rate would be made permanent. And the reasons are very simple: all the positive things that we predicted would happen happened. Consumption came back to Estonia on a massive basis and was better than predicted at a new, lower rate. Yes, it caused the state budget to sink, however, but the downfall that came from the reduction of diesel excise is about three times smaller than the downfall that came from the corona restrictions. So, yes, if we look at the general number in 2020, the amount of diesel excise tax received in Estonia was quite lower, but most of it was due to corona restrictions. And, of course, all these other hardest-measuring, positive side effects, i.e. improving the competitiveness of Estonian companies, the same Latvian-Lithuania transport companies, basically dumped entry into the Estonian market decreased significantly and the price increase for the final consumer decreased significantly. Estonia was the country with the fastest fall in prices in Europe in 2020, i.e. we had the highest fall in prices in all areas. Yes, because of the corona, there was a fall in prices throughout the euro area, but our fall in prices was the highest in comparison with all other countries. And the statistics clearly confirm that tax policy, or those excise duties on energy carriers that we did, played a very important role in this. The improvement of the labour market in these sectors as well. All the positive results we predicted were there. Now the following: when there was a rapid increase in excise duty in Estonia, both alcohol and fuel excise, as predicted and worse then, consumption moved to other countries and the receipts fell into the state budget. The RES adopted by the government has now predicted that despite the sharp rise in the price of diesel consumption in Estonia, it will continue to grow in the order of 5% per year. At the same time, we know that at the rate of excise duty to which it is intended to return, there was a decrease in consumption of around 5% per year. And the budget's coming down. If the government claims that diesel excise duty needs to be increased in order to improve the position of the state budget, they are living in some kind of delusion, because the revenue from the state budget with an increase in excise duty will not improve. Rather, it may be feared that the revenue from the state budget will decrease. If the sole objective is to balance the budget, the increase in excise duty will undermine the balance. But if the goal is not only that things in Estonia are cheap, that Estonian companies have the ability to compete with their neighbours, Latvians and Lithuanians, and if the goal is also to have a smaller carbon footprint, then diesel excise duty must not be increased. There will be no goods in Estonia, the goods will be transported here simply by trucks who drive home to Latvia or Lithuania for the night. That's what happens. We do not need less diesel in Estonia. It is simply refueled outside Estonia and taxes are also paid outside Estonia. So if I have one complaint to this bill, it is that the same rake is being re-taken, the current rate of continuation is only intended to be maintained for one year. In fact, a correction should be made to this draft, which will make the current rates of excise duty permanent. I have no belief that we will be able to change the law by May after the next Riigikogu elections in such a way that this automatic rise will not take place. That's not gonna happen. In that case, our diesel excise rate is gonna jump up and down. In any case, it must be made permanent and then this draft is very good.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U383	Let's move on to the next item: First reading of § 55 of the Supplementary Law Act 363 initiated by the Social Democratic Party Group and Riigikogu member Raimond Kaljulaid. The rapporteur is Heljo Pikhof, member of the Riigikogu. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U384	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Good fellows! Allow me to introduce a draft law supplementing § 55 of the Health Insurance Act initiated by the Social Democrats 363. First, what is the care allowance? Care allowance is a temporary incapacity benefit and is granted on the basis of the health insurance fund's maintenance sheet to a person who loses income subject to social tax due to the care of a sick child or other family member. The care allowance is paid on the basis of the recipient's daily income calculated on the basis of the income taxed on the social tax of the previous calendar year. The current procedure, where a parent who has left work after the end of the parental benefit receives only a minimum salary benefit for a sick child, is unfair to the family. The point is that 80% of the daily income paid to the insured for caring for the sick child is calculated on the basis of the social tax paid in the previous calendar year. However, no matter how much it is, no social tax is levied on parental benefits. Thus, the minimum wage is currently the basis for settlement. For many parents, this comes as a bad surprise, as did the journalist Piret Reiljan. He wrote in September 2019 that he was surprised to find that the state was paying 80% of the daily income charged by social tax in the previous calendar year for being on a care page with a sick child who had just returned to work, but the point is that parental pay is not subject to social tax. This means that, if the parent returns to work and, in the previous calendar year, did not earn a monthly salary exceeding the minimum wage in addition to the parental benefit, he or she will receive a minimum salary for the care of the sick child regardless of the amount of parental benefit received. He added that such a snip might blow a hole in the family budget. However, even if the parent has earned a premium higher than the minimum wage in the previous calendar year but has taken up work, for example in the middle or at the end of the year, the daily earnings earned over the last few months shall be distributed throughout the year. Thus, a parent who has not received his normal salary throughout the previous calendar year generally loses the amount of care allowance and this inevitably puts many families in an unexpectedly difficult situation. This unfair procedure for calculating care compensation applies from October 2002. Before that, the current principle of calculating the care allowance also applied, but was based on the last six months' remuneration. Thus, in 2002, the system became even more unfair to a working parent. In addition to the above, the care benefit rate was reduced from 100% to 80% in 2009 in order to save health insurance funds. But, as we well know, just as a young child at home adapts to the kindergarten and the collective, she gets sick very often and mother, sometimes her father, gets out of work. The data of the 2019 Health Insurance Fund only confirm this. The cost of care sheets for children under four years of age to the state was approximately 13 million euros, or more than half of the total cost of care compensation. But you can take care of a child up to 12 years of age and a disabled adolescent under 19 years of age, as well as other sick family members. If the baby is constantly ill and the mother is out of work, it can start with a sort of chain reaction. The parent will also be in a worse position next year if he or she needs a sick or care sheet, as welfare benefits will not be taxed on social security contributions either. Under the amendments to the draft regulation, a parent who has returned from parental leave who did not have social tax income or income less than the amount of parental benefit will receive 80% of the amount of parental benefit paid in the previous calendar year. If, in the previous calendar year, a person who has been on parental leave received, in addition to the parental benefit, income subject to social tax, which was higher than the amount of parental benefit, the payment of the care benefit shall be based on the income subject to social tax. Ladies and gentlemen, In November 2019, Minister Tanel Kiik stated that the current procedure, where the mother or father who returned to work after the termination of parental pay will receive compensation below the minimum wage on the basis of the sick child's maintenance sheet, has gone on track and that the Ministry has raised the issue and calculated the possible cost of changing the system. I would remind you that it was 2019. In an interview given to the newspaper Capital, Minister of Social Affairs Signe Riisalo has considered it right to reduce the vulnerability of women, particularly in terms of materiality and the risks of re-entering the labour market. The question now arises as to what would be the additional cost to the state budget. The additional cost to the state budget compared to the current situation would be EUR 2.4 million if the amount of care allowance would be 80% of their parental benefit if parents returned to work on parental leave. According to the information provided by the Health Insurance Fund in 2019, care sheets were taken to care for a child between 24477 and four years of age, which accounted for 34% of all children of the same age. This percentage has been taken into account for beneficiaries of parental benefit who are going to take care of them. The average number of days reimbursed per guardian of a child between one and four years of age was 18 days per year. Finally, according to the Socialists' supplementary proposal, the parental benefit would be paid or, if the person on parental leave has earned a higher income and is subject to social tax, a higher one. In short, compensation for the maintenance sheet should be more favourable to the family: 80% of the larger amount calculated. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U424	[[Remote connection]] Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, Our draft is rather short in terms of content and lines, but the work to prepare it was extremely long. On the basis of Article 11 (2) (3) of the Estonian Cultural Endowment Act, our draft law provides for the following ranking: the cultural centre of the heart of Tartu, the Cultural Quarter of Narva Kreenholm, the Arvo Pärt Music House in Rakvere and the extension of the current building of the National Opera in Tallinn. First of all, there were a number of things in the preparation of this law that deviated from the normal rhythm. The story started with that when we announced a competition (it happened in February 2020), the deadline for the submission of proposals was 30 April by which everyone could make their proposals publicly. But we all remember it was corona time. We thought it was almost over, and the deadline shifted to August 31st. I'm sure it was a good thing that this deadline could be shifted, because the first moods were so bad that even the opinion of the society here and there was that what cultural objects you were talking about, you better think about how to rebuild the Vanemuine Theatre into a hospital. I see, because the public had a great deal of attention against this draft and because some and others and third project performers needed opportunities to introduce their affairs more closely, and some felt that this should be done to one or another member of the Committee on Culture, so that it would really be as public as possible, we even organised a session. On 2 and 3 November, this was a session-free week of the Riigikogu, we had a presentation round where all the project performers who had not taken back their project had been invited. It was an online event and as a result the Committee on Culture looked, analysed and concluded that there was a very clear difference between the two groups of projects. This is how we found that we would continue to work on 11 better projects. Of course, in order to prevent all the questions that should come, although the explanatory memorandum has also been sought to make the most comprehensive and detailed possible, I would say that the main issue is whether all the threats have been removed and on what basis [decided], which were general principles, both journalists and colleagues have had. From the outset, the Commission has indeed established basic principles for itself. The first and unbreakable principle was that the object to be built must be important and contribute to the development of Estonian cultural life must be done in a sustainable way, not just for a moment. This was the most important criterion on which we had to base. Secondly, the members of the Commission suggested that the regional principle should be taken into account. Again and again, this statement that Estonia will not end when it comes out of Tallinn in any direction. So this was to be taken into account by the Commission. We also made an effort to read what the legislator had requested in his day to capture this spirit as accurately as possible. Two times we met with cultural capital, the second time with the Cultural Capital Council, because all the experience is crucial to not inventing the bike ourselves, or worse, driving from incompetent to a ditch. Both the smaller consultations and the formal meeting were with the Director-General of the Estonian Heritage Board, because we all know that there are a lot of heritage values, which are usually hidden under the soil in the sweetest parts of the cities. Today, it is not the case that the same rooster euro comes into play and is immediately put into play, but, of course, different financial schemes must always be used in the interests of efficiency. Nobody appreciates it too good that the matter started in 1997 will finally be paid in the first half of 2022. Therefore, the limit of why four is maximum and should not be taken as five, six or eight is partly a question of responsibility, or if you want to do so, it is said that there is also a question of madness of magnitude. After all, we could have made a ranking of all 11 of them and said that there are now a series of cultural buildings for the next 80 years. We tried to reach an optimal solution.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U492	Thank you, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Ladies and gentlemen, 25 years ago, on 5 November 1996 Kaljo Kiisa and Tõnu Tepandi submitted a draft decision on 11 September 386 on the construction of the Estonian Academy of Music, the Estonian Art Museum and the Estonian National Museum and its deadlines. The first building of the Estonian Academy of Music and Theatre was completed by the prescribed deadline, but only in 2019, Kumu was opened in 2006 and in ERM there was a change of location and project, and the building was built almost ten years later from the decision of the Riigikogu. Point 2 of the draft decision at that time deserves attention. I quote: "The financing of the construction works will be determined on the basis of this Decision by the annual state budget law, bearing in mind the financial possibilities of a given budget year. The Government of the Republic has the task of seeking the financing of the construction works mentioned in the Act outside the state budget and of seeking the assistance of international organisations and foreign countries. " It was this task that, when Kumu was built, the finance minister Siim Kallas and Chancellor Margus Allikmaa of the Ministry of Culture came up with the idea of finding additional sources to fund cultural objects. On 13 March 2002, the Riigikogu passed the Gambling Tax Act, which stipulated that 46% of the proceeds of the tax will go to cultural capital and 64% to cultural buildings. An additional source of funding for cultural buildings was created, in addition to supporting culture already functioning through cultural capital. On 9 October 2002, the Cultural Capital Act was supplemented by Article 11 (2) (4) of the Act: "The Cultural Capital Council distributes the amounts received on the basis of the Gambling Tax Act in accordance with the ranking of the construction and renovation of important national cultural buildings, approved by the Riigikogu's decision, while supporting up to two objects at the same time." The previous one was only a brief view of the history of building cultural objects with the support of cultural capital. Today we have a draft decision with 387 key cultural objects. How did the Commission arrive at this list and this ranking? In the course of the nearly two-year consideration and hearings, have these objects considered the importance of Estonian culture as a whole, encouraging cooperation between cultural areas, regional justification, beneficiaries and beneficiaries of the cultural object, potential costs, co-financing and partners, the cost of further maintenance of the object and its carriers? Yes, 30 proposals were received by the Commission by 30 August, and indeed, the public sittings on 2 and 3 November gave an overview of the presentations of 27 sites. Of these, each member of the Commission had to choose the 10 projects most satisfying the criteria and the pre-selection order of 11 objects, four of which are before us today, as the law states. The call for proposals was announced last February. Proposals were expected for buildings that are important for Estonian culture as a whole, contribute to ensuring the continuity of Estonian culture, including national culture, and to the holistic development of the cultural field, and encourage cooperation between cultural fields. That's what we agreed on. The proposals also requested the regional justification of the building and its potential users and participants. The appointment of national cultural buildings was asked, where possible, to be accompanied by an estimate of the estimated cost of the works, information on potential co-financing and partners in addition to cultural capital and a clear vision of how to organise further maintenance of the completed building. In the light of the special situation that has hit us, the deadline for submitting proposals was extended and the Committee on Culture received a total of 30 proposals by September. There was an analysis of the proposals and a request for more information, and all interested parties were met at public meetings. By the end of autumn, the Committee on Culture selected 11 objects that entered the second round. In my opinion, the process was transparent and accessible to the public. Many candidates introduced their plan in addition to the Commission in the public. The vote then took place in the Committee on Culture. As agreed in the Committee on Culture, each member of the Commission had four equal votes, i.e. each member selected 11 of the objects that had gained his attention. After that, the votes were counted and the first four were selected from which candidates had become the most votes. The members of the Committee on Culture then voted once more between the four selected objects in order to set the ranking. In that order, the objects were included in the bill. So why did they form the same line of tension? Just to be able to read the will of the majority of the MPs. This can be accepted or not, however, the majority of the members of the Committee on Culture have decided on precisely this order. This means that the details of Tartu and Narva objects will be very quick. In essence, according to the law, up to two objects can be financed at a time and so the first two can be moved forward in the coming year. ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U506	We are going to the fifth item on the agenda: the third reading of the draft law amending the Code of Misdemeanour Procedure and the Traffic Act initiated by the Committee on Legal Affairs. Do you want to negotiate? It's what you want. I'm opening negotiations. I would like to ask Urve Tiidus, representative of the Reform Party Group, for the speech chair of the Riigikogu. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U507	Respectables! At first, I would like to say that this law does not change the world, but the world has inspired itself to do so. The law amending the Code of Misdemeanour Procedure and the Traffic Act reflects in a creative way the effects of ever accelerating communication and haste on our traffic behaviour and trying to control this constant haste. There is, of course, a need for restraint in order to reduce the risk taking on the roads. The alternative and innovative solution for positive effects on traffic behaviour is the same draft calming down. The draft started a brief test on Estonian roads by the Ministry of the Interior last year, when a new tool was tried to control speeders in cooperation with the Police and Border Guard Board. In particular, instead of paying a fine to passengers, it was offered to immediately choose a 45-minute rest stop. By supporting the draft today, we are giving the opportunity to formally implement this promising method into practice. Yes, the police will be able to direct the speeder to continue driving by the road for 45 minutes and, in the long run, perhaps to continue to act safer on the road, both for themselves and for other passengers. Just as people are different, different measures will have different effects, as our committee chairman said. The imposition of a fine on speed limiters does not always produce the desired result and the options are welcome. However, I would point out that the choice of a calming stop cannot be relied upon by those who are at very high risk. In the course of the Commission's procedure and involvement, the details of the law, the right of appeal, technical needs, the possibility of proof were clarified, unless the speed-over can wait 45 minutes, and so on. The draft and amendments received unanimous support from the Committee on Legal Affairs. The time dimension in this law can also be spoken of in terms of the effectiveness of the infringement and the subsequent leverage. The effect is considered by the theorists to be the more effective the more immediate the sanction is. In this sense, a stopover in controlling traffic behaviour is at least theoretically a good method. Which one of us doesn't know that time is money. Its author is Benjamin Franklin, the great 18th century. If the alternative cost is generally considered to be economics, then also in traffic behaviour, the driver is unconsciously doing these calculations often, with an à la race 20 kilometres per hour faster, I arrive seven minutes earlier, and vice versa. So the alternative cost in the context of traffic behaviour represents a potential loss of revenue for an individual when he chooses another behaviour instead of one. Perhaps when this law comes into force, it may be too much time to buy, but the police may find themselves losing more time. And compared to the loss of money and the penalty: the penalty money can be earned back, but it can't be earned back. This draft has received unanimous support from the Committee on Legal Affairs, which was also the initiator, as well as from two readings in this Chamber. Yes, today we are giving the green road to this innovative alternative so that it can be put into practice and that our roads will be a little safer again. Thank you so much for listening.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U508	Thank you. I would like to ask Jaanus Karilaidi, representative of the Estonian Central Party Group, for a speech chair in the Riigikogu.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U509	Ladies and gentlemen, Good manager! I'm going to go over 216 cores of the bill. First of all, I would like to thank Urve Tiidu, who was in charge of the draft in the Commission and did so very masterfully during all three readings. The draft introduces the possibility of using a single measure of restraint. The measure is intended to affect those who have exceeded the maximum permitted speed of up to 20 kilometres per hour. With the introduction of this measure, the ADR operator will be given the opportunity to direct the person after the suspension of his vehicle to the area for a specified period of time to wait for the journey to continue. A person aiming to calm down must wait 45 minutes with the vehicle before he/she is allowed to continue moving. The police will have the right to decide, when the infringement is fixed and when a short procedure is initiated, whether to impose a fine on the person or to exempt the person from payment of the penalty if the person agrees to comply with the terms of the sanction provided for in the law. This will make it possible to assess whether the person who was the subject of the infringement would in the future be subject to a fine or a sanction. If the person accepts the sanction, he or she shall be given a decision in a short procedure stating that he or she will not have to pay a fine if the sanction is enforced. If the person accepts the sanction but does not comply with it, he or she shall be informed that he or she is obliged to pay the fine provided for in the short procedure. In all cases, it is ensured that a person can appeal to the courts. The primary incentive among alternative traffic-related leverage activities is this measure because the number of traffic offences remains high, and it is important to respond to it in the most effective way possible precisely in the event of the first and least serious breach. Relaxation will therefore create another possibility to influence the person's traffic behaviour and compliance with speed limits at an early stage, when the violations committed by the person and the mistakes made in traffic behaviour are not yet so serious as to lead to a more severe sanction. It is an indicative, proactive and educational measure. Special thanks to Henry Timberg and Veiko Kombusaar of the Ministry of the Interior and Markus Kärner of the Ministry of Justice. Amazing but true: all five political parties represented in Parliament agreed on this draft. Still green, it relaxes.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U510	Thank you. I don't see any more calls. I will close the negotiations and start preparing for the final vote. Can we go to the final vote? I will put draft 216 to the final vote. Please take a stand and vote.
