ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U540	[[Remote connection]] Honourable Members of the Riigikogu! This is a very complicated law and the Committee on Constitutional Affairs deepened it in several sessions. As mentioned at the meeting of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, but also in the Chamber at first reading of this draft law, we also addressed the National Defence Committee to find out what the opinion of the National Defence Committee on this draft is. It is recalled that this is a cluster proposal concerning the establishment of a national reserve centre on the basis of the current liquid fuel reserve agency and, on the other hand, the transfer of civil crisis management policy from the Ministry of Internal Affairs to the State Chancellery. The National Defence Committee presented a highly recognised and comprehensive assessment. In short, it can be said that, in its observations, the Committee on National Defence pointed out that cross-ministerial coordination, the leading role of the Prime Minister, is necessary. This law is expected to provide for this, indeed, by not mentioning the Prime Minister specifically. However, the Committee on National Defence stressed that the clarity of the emergency management mechanism requires serious attention. It should be stressed that the transfer of competences also requires the existence of competent officials and necessary capacities in the State Chancellery. The National Defence Committee has raised a number of questions, including the need to analyse whether the Rescue Board really needs the competence to implement the specific national supervision measures provided for in the Law Enforcement Act in order to carry out a large-scale evacuation with the Police and Border Guard Board. However, the Commission acknowledged that in essence the proposed amendments to the draft law were necessary. The National Defence Committee discussed but did not reach agreement on a number of issues. For example, the members of the National Defence Committee, Mr Kunnas, and Mr Laneman, felt that the draft would create an ineffective governance model for crisis management. The members of the above-mentioned Commission also considered that the planned crisis management cannot be collective and that the area of emergency law cannot be extended to prepare for a threat to national security and constitutional order. In addition, the Committee on Constitutional Affairs received a number of opinions on these issues. The Chamber of Commerce considered it necessary that the Emergency Act provides for food production and food safety as a vital service. The association of pharmaceutical wholesalers pointed out that representatives from different sectors should also be on the board of this stock centre in an analogous way to those of fuel companies in the liquid fuel reserve agency. The oil association sent a letter to the Commission stressing that the other economic activities of the stock centre to be set up would not be subsidised to fuel consumers by means of a reserve levy on liquid fuel. The Commission invited representatives of these institutions to its meeting, which looked at specific amendments tabled by members of the Riigikogu. From the amendments tabled by the members of the Riigikogu, if you look at the table of the amendments, first, the EKRE Group tabled amendments, the substance of which is that it wishes to maintain the current competence of coordinating the crisis management of the Ministry of Internal Affairs. Therefore, these amendments are essentially intended as a cut-off amendment to the draft law in order to maintain the existing legal regime in general. The Isamaa Group has also tabled an amendment whose core is to exclude the transfer of competences of the Ministry of the Interior and other ministries to the State Chancellery. In addition, the question raised in the Commission's proposal from the National Defence Committee related to the government's request for special rights to the Rescue Board, such as search and detention, to carry out a large-scale evacuation. However, the Commission unanimously took the view that it was not justified in this draft to extend the individual competences so as to facilitate evacuation to the Rescue Board. The Commission did not support any of the amendments proposed by these two opposition parties, but, in consensus, supported one of the Commission's amendments aimed at eliminating the granting of additional powers to the Rescue Board from this draft law. During the debate, when looking at the issues that arose, a huge number of specialists had been invited to the meeting of the Commission on 4 May. The Commission carried out extensive work, with both public officials and representatives of the interest groups I referred to, who had also raised their concerns in writing. During the Commission's debate, the authors of the amendments were heard and the opinions of the National Defence Committee were heard again. Mr Kunnas presented the views of the members of the committee expressing a different opinion in the Committee on National Defence and the views of the majority of Mr Raidma's committee on this matter. With regard to the issues that emerged in the wider debate in the Commission, it can be pointed out from stakeholders that, as the initiator of the draft of the State Chancellery, the Chamber of Agriculture indicated that food production and food safety should also be a vital service. The Secretary of State, on behalf of the Government, said that the government has given ministries tasks to ensure food production and food safety. It must be made clear whether this is a vital service or not. Hence, the government wonders whether food is necessary or whether food is not vital to human functioning and existence. But in this draft view, the government does not plan any changes in the light of this issue. Then there was also a hearing with the representative of the oil company concerning the definition of the Council. The position of the representatives of the government is that the members of the council will be appointed by the nomination committee, which will follow the principles set out in the law. In conclusion, no representative of the so-called reserve holders is intended to be appointed to that meeting in the event of the formation or transformation of this company from the liquid fuel reserve agency. As regards the indication of whether the future economic activities of the Estonian Stock Exchange Centre will be cross-subsidised through a reserve payment of liquid fuel to be collected from fuel consumers, amounting to EUR 5 million per year, the government representative, Mrs Talving, stressed that the new spare groups are not subject to a back-up payment obligation, but, in the case of new spare groups, the financing of stocks is made from the state budget. For this purpose, co-financing is planned for the Estonian Stock Centre since its launch. As I pointed out, the authors of the amendments presented the amendments they had tabled. The government representative did not support these amendments. At its second meeting, the Commission also discussed the draft Act amending the Emergency Act. The fundamental issues of crisis and crisis management have also been overcome. Here it became clear what the EKRE amendment wanted to delete, namely the first question that now also the attack on constitutional order, let us say, the coup d'état and the issues of state of emergency will be governed in the future by this law as a 'base' or, in the preparatory phase, by the Emergency Act. This provoked a very serious controversy within the Commission. The state of emergency is still a very specific state of threat to constitutional order, but the emergency in general is still linked to an epidemic, a natural disaster or a disaster, i.e. a completely different nature. Consequently, with this law, we must recognise that some members of the Commission saw this border with a very clear special regime, where there is an attack against constitutional order and a threat of constitutional order, we are beginning to blur by nature. Secondly, there was also the question that we are now trying to change certain individual competences in the middle of a crisis, one emergency situation. Is that appropriate? All the more so since there has been a national defence law in Parliament for a long time, which provides for a more holistic approach. The Secretary of State, on behalf of the Government, confirmed that precisely so, this is the repair of one facet, followed by much more extensive changes to regulate this issue. Although the Commission did not support the amendments tabled by the EKRE Group in order to define the Estonian Stock Centre as a specific implementing rule of the law, the Commission's minutes also highlighted and included that when we talk about the design of a new state-owned company from the surface of the Estonian Liquid Fuel Supply Agency, it should definitely be called the Estonian Stock Centre. I hope that the same attitude was felt by the representatives of the government who admitted this will of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. As a summary, this was the course of discussion on this draft law. In fact, if you point out the essence of this draft law, it is a package of two separate themes called Brezhnev. One issue is the creation of a reserve centre and the other is, as I said, the transfer of the legal or, say, general coordination of an emergency from the Ministry of the Interior to the State Chancellery, which concerns the preparation for crises and the implementation of this policy. This is certainly accompanied, in addition to the relocation of some people, by different results in the administrative practice of Estonia, including the fact that the State Chancellery will prepare laws, produce laws. It is unclear who will then report these laws, whether these laws are being passed by the Minister authorised by the Prime Minister or by the Secretary of State, or by the Prime Minister. The controversy in the Commission was also caused by who is actually in charge of these things. The draft law speaks of collective leadership and says that the whole government is in charge, but there is no definition of personal political leadership responsibility, which was also highlighted by some members of the National Defence Committee in their expertise or opinion. There was undoubtedly one round of questions in the Commission that raised concerns about the fact that we will be prepared for this under the Emergency Act in the event of an attack against the state of emergency, against constitutional order. We have just said that if we think back that the emergency situation is not announced because it would be bad, and we have an emergency situation, we can also say that the normal or, say, the emergency situation is actually a state of emergency. These are the issues that arose in the Commission. The members of the Commission were generally good in my opinion. I think everyone contributed to their views. And the most important thing is that we were able to make decisions. We never had any conflict of role, nor did we develop such an internal vacuum or a crisis in the work of the Committee on Management. So I think that the chairman of the committee did his job in full satisfaction. Thank you.

(U.R.) in preparation for a threat to the security and constitutional order of the state, in times of increased readiness for defence, state of emergency and martial law, in so far as other laws do not provide otherwise. According to this bill, the threat to the basic order alone, its preparation in the future, will take place under the Emergency Act. This Act lays down the basis, conditions, competence of the bodies, remedies to be applied and the rights and obligations of persons in order to eliminate the threat to the constitutional order of Estonia. Now, about this bill that we are dealing with here, I have a very interesting point. First of all, § 1 (2) is amended to apply this law in preparation for a threat to national security and constitutional order, in so far as other laws do not provide otherwise. But in this case under trial today, it remains in a very grey area, it is unclear who will announce whether it is the Government of the Republic, the State Chancellery or some other third. There is no legal clarity in the law. It gives rise to disputes, to different interpretations, to vague definitions, and therefore, in my opinion, it does not suffer any criticism of the correlation of laws, and also, let us say, any criticism. But it seems to me that this is not to be done, and it is also, as we have heard, very urgent in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. I wonder where we're going. There's a crisis, is it a threat to constitutional order? Is it necessary for the government to have any special powers to start implementing and putting such restrictions and obstacles to the realisation of the fundamental rights of the people under the name and auspices of preparation for the threat of constitutional order, because that is what it says? I believe that this law is a serious threat to the freedoms and rights of the people enshrined in the Constitution of the Republic of Estonia, and it cannot be adopted in this form under any circumstances. So the task of the inventory centre is either to manage it or to collect it yourself somewhere. And the supply center doesn't touch defense supplies either, so it's getting out of the way. That's one, and I don't think there's been any argument about that. I understand that there are different opinions here, it is a question of options. Another fundamental change is that the preparation for civil crises according to this draft will be transferred from the Ministry of the Interior to the Government of the Republic. There has indeed been a great deal of debate about this. And if we take this draft, I would like to read this point to you in advance, § 4: "The development and implementation of a state crisis regulation policy will be led by the Government of the Republic. " That is the text of the draft. [The Emergency Act shall be amended as follows: Section 4 shall be amended and worded as follows]: "§ 4. Coordination of crisis management. The development and implementation of the state's crisis regulation policy is managed by the Government of the Republic. " Did you notice any difference in that wording? One word or one point or a comma? It's one of those laws that's taken over by one of them. And the previous government not only discussed it, but initiated it, this bill was brought to the Riigikogu. Everything's changed now overnight. Well, if things have to be different, ladies and gentlemen, it would be quite appropriate that you first admit that you were wrong at the time. Of course, we can also change our positions, but change our positions when it changes in the position of the seats, well, I think it's not very honest and very correct. I admit that is not very common. It is not very common to be placed under the State Chancellery and the Government of the Republic. But this is not a first. When we talk about preparing for security crises, it is already under the Government of the Republic today. The preparations for security crises are under the Government of the Republic. No one has contested this, saying that the problem here is that the government is a collegial body. And yet the government is run by the Prime Minister. I say that this level of responsibility has been raised considerably higher than it is today. Today it is at the level of the Ministry of the Interior and the Minister of the Interior. Now, under this bill, it will be transferred to the State Chancellery and the Government of the Republic and the Prime Minister will be responsible. Surprise-surprise, once again a little unconventional, also a little surprising for me: these possible drafts must be protected by the Prime Minister of the Republic of Estonia in this regard. It's not very common. But once again, I am absolutely convinced that the Prime Minister can do this. Somehow to say it's like anonymous now, the Prime Minister will be replaced by the Minister of the Interior. And this level of responsibility is many times higher than it is today. What else? A colleague said that crisis management is becoming much more unclear. How can crisis management become much more unclear with this draft if it does not even concern crisis management for a cent? There is no word for crisis management. Once again, preparing for civilian crises is what goes into the hands of the Government of the Republic and the State Chancellery, not crisis management. On the side of crisis management, both ministries and other institutions will continue to retain their responsibilities, just as they have so far. We were looking for a replacement for that word. In fact, this management means, above all, that, among other things, the State Chancellery and the Government of the Republic will be given the task of coordinating exercises in emergency management. The same word "leads" applies more to this. But this country's crisis regulation policy is nothing to do with governance. Perhaps, ladies and gentlemen, once again: neither the Government of the Republic nor the Prime Minister will lead the crisis; there will be no change here. ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U644	We will proceed to our 10th item today, which is the first reading of the draft law amending the Act on the Election of the European Parliament and the Law on the Organisation of Local Government, initiated by the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, 389 (the participation of a Member of the European Parliament in the work of the local government council). Toomas Kivimägi, Chairman of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, will present this to us. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U645	Mr President, thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, I would ask you not to leave both the hall and the screens, because this bill is no less exciting than the previous one. This draft has been initiated by the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. I think it is important to point out immediately that this is such a good draft that, after the Committee on Constitutional Affairs had initiated this draft, there was a huge struggle to see who was really the initiator of this draft. On the opposition side and on the coalition side, both parties wanted to take the credit, but I assure and repeat that this draft was initiated by the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. I am also particularly pleased to say that the Committee on Constitutional Affairs did so in a consensual manner. To be very precise, all members of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs who took part in the vote voted in favour of initiating this draft. Secondly, it is also important to clarify that seven out of nine members of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs took part in the vote. The purpose of this draft is to remove the restrictions on members of the European Parliament elected from Estonia not to participate in the work of the local rural municipality or city council. Members of the European Parliament may stand as candidates in municipal elections, but they cannot be members of local municipal or urban councils at the same time as [member of the European Parliament]. If that is what they want, then they will have to give up the mandate of a Member of the European Parliament, and I think it would be a little bit much to want. It is important to note that in this case one important objective is to apply an analogy to the members of the Riigikogu. Quite a number of years ago, the parliament approved that members of the Riigikogu may also belong to local government councils at the same time. I am pleased to note that, in fact, this law has landed on a very fertile surface. As far as I know, 48 members of the Riigikogu are also members of the local rural municipality or city council, so this is not just a formality, but on the contrary, this [option] has actually been very basically used. And maybe another important line. It's a little emotional, but I was very disappointed when Postimees called it a decoy duck bill in one of the web sites. It must have been taken down very quickly, and it was soon changed. In my opinion, this is quite the opposite. I would argue that, while MEPs may stand for municipal and city councils but may not belong to them, the word 'decoup duck' could be used in this case. However, if Parliament approves the draft which the Committee on Constitutional Affairs initiated, then it would be extremely inappropriate to use the word 'decoup party' for MEPs who are candidates for election to municipal and city councils. Now another very important substantive argument. The European Parliament Election Act was adopted already in 2002, two years before Estonia joined the European Union. I am saying that we are actually making a correction to the 2002 errors because, in fact, there is no justification for the restriction by which MEPs are not allowed to belong to local councils until today. The Supreme Court has found that restrictions on the exercise of the passive electoral right of the council, the acquisition and exercise of the mandate, including the power of a council member, early termination, suspension and restoration, may be imposed in order to protect particularly weighty legal values. The question immediately arises of what are these particularly weighty legal values, which is why they have not been granted this right until the moment Parliament approves this draft. The Supreme Court also says that restrictions must not deter individuals or groups with real support from the council, and such restrictions would damage the foundations of local governments so that the representative body cannot become sufficiently representative. In fact, I admit that there has been a debate on why, in 2002, when the European Parliament's election law was adopted, there was no debate on this subject, I dare say that this is rather an unjustified restriction. But if we approve this draft in this Chamber, we will actually correct the error that has not been corrected since 2002. What is actually very strange is that if today a Member of the European Parliament cannot be a mayor or a mayor, then a surprise-surprism, according to the law in force today, a Member of the European Parliament may be a member of a rural municipality government or city government, not a mayor or mayor, but he may be a member, which is still completely absurd. He may be a member of a municipality government or city government, but he may not be a member of a council. He may not be a member of a representative body, but he may be a member of an executive body. With this bill, we are also making a correction to the fact that we are losing the right of a Member of the European Parliament to belong to a municipality or city government. Including, of course, the principle that he cannot be either an elder or mayor remains valid. It is also important to note here that the draft law is scheduled to enter into force on the day following the election of local government councils. The next regular municipal elections are known to take place on 17 October and this bill is scheduled to enter into force on 18 October, the day following the regular municipal elections. Therefore, it is not with retroactive effect for the current municipal or city council, but MEPs will then be entitled to belong to it from 18 October. What else can I say? Maybe in two words, I'll tell you what the protocol says. I consider it important to point out, in the interests of the truth, that this draft has been initiated by the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, but the original text of this draft was drafted by Olivia Talle, Legal Adviser to the Regional Administrative Policy Department of the Ministry of Finance, who also attended the meeting of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs and gave reasons and presented the draft and mostly the same points of view that I spoke of here. Ms Taltude also pointed out the principle that, like members of the Riigikogu, members of the European Parliament are not remunerated for participating in the work of a council member. Perhaps, just as members of the Riigikogu do not receive any remuneration for participating in the work of the council, the same principle applies to the members of the European Parliament if they are members of the municipal or city council. At the same time, they, like the members of the Riigikogu, are still entitled to compensation for expenses incurred in carrying out the duties of the council, such as travel, accommodation and other travel expenses, I believe and hope that most of us will not use it, and that the Members of the European Parliament will not use it, but they will be entitled to compensation on the basis of the documents submitted. They are covered by the rural municipality's or city's budget. 11 May was the date on which the Committee on Constitutional Affairs initiated this draft. Maybe some lines that are important to point out. In particular, Paul Puustusmaa said that the Estonian Conservative People's Party has always been against the so-called two-chair law. It is now difficult to say what the position of the EKRE Group on this matter is. At the meeting of the Commission, Mart Helme, a full member of the Commission, was presently a member of Mr Grünthal's alternate, who said that the EKRE Group has no fundamental opposition at present to this amendment. However, it is also important to note that Urmas Reinsalu said that the Isamaa Group discussed this amendment and that the group had different views on the matter and that no final position was reached during the discussions. It cannot therefore be said whether or not the Isamaa Group supports the initiation of the draft. The question also arose as to how important this bill really is when it is passed as a law. In fact, this is not of significant weight because today there are a total of 1729 parish and city council members. If seven of them are Members of the European Parliament, then in fact, I riddled out, it is 0.4%. In fact, there is no significant impact in the 'numbers' world, but I hope that on the substance side it will be a very important complement to the competence and the link between European positions and local authorities. So I am absolutely convinced that this will certainly significantly improve the quality of the debate. Then the decisions were made, and I'll read them. First, on 11 May, it was decided to initiate a law amending the European Parliament Election Act and the Local Government Organisation Act. The decision was consensual. Mart Helme, Toomas Kivimägi, Andrei Korobeinik, Paul Puustusmaa, Martin Repinski, Marko Storm and Mart Võrguship took part in the vote. These names are repeating, so I'm not going to read them from now on. Seven of the nine members of the Commission took part in the vote; two of them did not participate. Secondly, it was decided to appoint Toomas Kivimägi, the chairman of the committee, as the representative of the Constitutional Committee in the Riigikogu when dealing with the draft. This decision was also consensual, and the same seven people voted in favour of it. Thirdly, it was decided to propose a draft on the plenary agenda on 19 May 2021 and this decision was also consensual. It was then discussed by the Committee on Constitutional Affairs [the draft and] also took procedural decisions on 17 May 2021. As the substantive discussion of this draft was held at the initiation of the draft, the draft was not discussed in substance at this meeting of the Commission. It is important to note that Urmas Reinsalu reiterated that he is not taking part in the vote, where the committee decides whether or not to propose to the Riigikogu plenary that the first reading of the draft be stopped. Then procedural decisions were made. Firstly, to propose that the first reading of the draft be stopped. This decision was consensual, with Kalle Grünthal, Toomas Kivimägi, Andrei Korobeinik, Oudekki Loone, Jevgeni Ossinovski, Paul Puustusmaa and Mart Võrguship. Secondly, it was decided to propose a deadline of 27 May 2021 for amendments. This decision was also consensual, and here, in addition to the previous ones, Urmas Reinsalu [voted in favour]. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U678	Today's 11th item is the first reading of the draft law amending the collective agreement and other laws initiated by the Government of the Republic. Tanel Kiik, Minister of Health and Labour, will give a presentation. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U679	Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I will briefly introduce the draft law amending the collective agreement law and other laws 383. The purpose of the amendments to the law is to supplement the law on collective agreements and to align the regulation on the extension of the terms of collective agreements with the freedom to conduct a business protected by the Constitution. At the same time, it is important to ensure the capacity of social partnership and collective inclusion. This amendment of the law is due to the fact that the current regulation is incomplete in the view of the preconditions for the extension of the collective agreement and the criteria for its application and, consequently, it is possible for a small group to agree on the extension of obligations to the whole sector. In it, both the Chancellor of Justice and the Supreme Court have seen a disproportionate restriction on the freedom to conduct a business. The most important thing here is to mention the Supreme Court decision of 15 June 2020 number 2-18-7821, which draws attention to this same issue. More specifically, the bill amends the law of the collective agreement in such a way that the conditions governing the extension of the collective agreement will apply in the future. The extension of the terms of the collective agreement can then be agreed as one of the parties to the agreement by a trade union or union association whose members make up 15% of the employees in the industry or who have at least 500 members, and by an employers' association or association which employs at least 40% of the employees in the sector as the other party. Before extending the terms of the collective agreement, the negotiating parties have an obligation to inform employees and employers and to consult those in respect of whom these conditions are to be extended. The expanded conditions of the collective agreement will be monitored and the extended terms of the collective agreement will be published by the Minister responsible for the field, he will then be Minister for Social Affairs, Minister for Health and Labour or any other option for the future. The draft also amends the Law on Trade Unions and on the Employee Trustees Act, specifying the employer's obligation to give time off from working time to carry out the duties of the trustee, even in the event of a large number of trustees, in which case several different trustees have been selected in the company or organisation. The Employment Contracts Act is also amended by increasing the compensation payable in the event of nullity of the employment relationship in a situation where the employment relationship has been cancelled without a legal basis with a pregnant woman, a maternity leave or a worker's representative. Similarly, the Civil Service Act is amended by increasing the compensation payable in the event of nullity of a service relationship in similar situations, i.e. where a service relationship is cancelled without a legal basis with a representative of a pregnant person, a maternity leave, a child under seven years of age or a civil servant. Both laws raise the compensation rate from six months to 12 months, when compensation is paid in cases where the court or labour dispute committee establishes the nullity of the cancellation of a contract of employment with a pregnant worker, a worker entitled to maternity leave or with a representative of the officials, respectively. Among other things, some technical changes are made to the collective agreement law as well as to the working conditions of workers posted to Estonia, which do not entail changes to the current practice. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U680	Thank you. It is now possible to ask questions. Oudekki Loone, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U681	[[Remote connection]] Thank you. Thank you, Minister, for this very good review. But I have a question. ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U682	[[Remote connection]] Thank you for your question. Indeed, at the moment, the aim has been for such unfounded, unfounded cases to have a sufficiently large social guarantee for the trustee or, as I previously pointed out, for the various situations in which a person has been deprived of contract either when pregnant or during maternity leave [right] collectively. In certain cases, it can be said that reinstating could be appropriate and possible, in certain cases it may not be practically adequate or real if we are talking about a person who has already gone on parental leave or in some other situation after the early termination of service as a pregnant woman. In this case, we need to look legally at what the options are. Of course, the person on maternity leave also has the right to retain his or her job, but the current idea and agreement reached with employers and trade unions is to make such cases as little as possible by raising compensation. After all, the aim and goal is that situations such as those where, for some inadequate reason, a contract of employment is terminated should be minimal. All forms and possibilities of rehabilitation are an additional debate that is difficult to imagine in practice in terms of achieving a normal supportive working climate.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U685	[[Remote connection]] Thank you very much for your question. Of course, setting any limit always raises questions, disputes and debates. The reason why we have even started to change this law is, as I referred to earlier, the feedback from the Chancellor of Justice and the Supreme Court and the specific decision of the Supreme Court concerning the extension of collective agreements. These numbers have been agreed in tripartite negotiations with trade unions and employers and, of course, they may have a different understanding of what is the right and precise number, but it is important that a certain representation is guaranteed and also the specific condition of the legislative extension is fixed. These numbers are substantial enough in this view to allow, in more common cases, these collective agreements to be extended to include, for example, the medical sector, the transport sector, and, at the same time, to mitigate those fears and threats that have been feared that the extension would not have been carried out by a very small group. So that is where these numbers have come from and, of course, it is appropriate for Parliament to continue the debate and the debate on them.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U692	No further questions. Thank you to the Minister. I call Siret Kotka, representative of the Social Affairs Committee, as rapporteur.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U693	[[Remote connection]] Honourable members of the Riigikogu! The Social Affairs Committee dealt with the draft law amending the collective agreement law and other laws at its meeting on 10 May. As is the case here today in the plenary hall of the Riigikogu, Minister Tanel Kiik introduced the content and purpose of the Bill 383 of the Collective Agreement. Members of the Commission also had questions, and I will mark and touch them a little bit. The members of the committee were interested in how it was regulated by the trustee's leisure time so that the trustee would not abuse it. It was answered that the division of the working time of the trustees would be agreed by the parties and that the control would be carried out through the employees who chose the trustee. If the trustee does not perform his duties, employees will have the opportunity to choose a new trustee. There was an interest as to whether the rights of the trustee would be extended to the Chairman of the trade union. It was answered that the trustee could be elected either by the employees or by the trade union, and in some cases the trustee and the union chairman could overlap. The aim is to provide each trustee with free time. In addition, there was an interest in the source of remuneration for the trustees when the tasks are performed during working hours. It was answered that the time given to the trustee to carry out his duties was remunerated. The following decisions were taken: to propose that the draft be put on the plenary agenda on 19 May inclusive (consensually), to propose that the first reading be closed (consensually), to propose that the deadline for amendments be set at 4 p.m. (consensually) and to appoint Siret Kotka as rapporteur (consensually). ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U695	Today's item No 12 is the first reading of Bill 381 of the Law amending the Water Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. The rapporteur is Tõnis Mölder, Minister for the Environment. Please! No, you don't have to put on a mask.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U696	Thank you, Mr President. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! You have received a draft law amending the Water Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. The purpose of the draft law is, in particular, to reduce red tape, to ensure better legal clarity and, at the same time, to correct or supplement the provisions [to resolve problems] which have then arisen in the course of implementing the codified Water Act, which entered into force in October 2019. More important changes. First of all, the Water Act removes the obligation to submit a plan for spreading liquid manure, which concerns about 40 of Estonia's largest dairy farmers and pig farmers. As the information to be included in the manure spreading plan does not clearly reflect the activities to be carried out and all data on the actual use of manure must be recorded in the farm book, it is not reasonable to require a separate information document to be submitted to the Environmental Board prior to application. This will reduce the administrative burden. The obligation to submit a notification for grazing in a water protection zone, to be submitted by livestock farmers grazing more than 10 livestock units for grazing in a water protection zone, is also excluded. Around 300 notifications on grazing in water protection zones are submitted annually to the Environmental Board. The amendments to the legislation have no negative environmental impact since other provisions of the Water Act lay down requirements for the use of liquid manure as fertiliser and for grazing in the water protection zone of animals. Thus, the amendment reduces the administrative burden on farmers as well as the bureaucracy of the Environmental Board. In addition, the law regulates the provisions relating to the designation of nitrate sensitive areas. The right to designate a nitrate sensitive area is, according to today's law, transferred from the Government of the Republic to the Minister for the Environment and the definitions of water protection objects in nitrate sensitive areas, such as karst phenomena, are also specified to ensure better legal clarity. One amendment also concerns the provisions on the management of waste water collection and treatment, which specify the possibility of using waste water collection tanks in high pollution waste water collection areas. The high pollution load is typically more than 2 000 consumers [in the waste water collection area]. According to the amendments, a leak-proof collection tank may also be used in such areas, provided that the environment can be protected equally by the use of the word 'equivalently' collective sewerage. Thus, according to the amendments, the joint sewerage with more than 2000 consumers may not be built on waste water collection areas if it would not be beneficial for the environment or if it would be too costly to set up. The formulation of the term "water body' laid down in the current law has led to different understandings and thus uneven application of the law. We will specify this term within the framework of this law and make it uniformly understandable. The Water Act also introduces amendments to the provisions relating to deepening, according to which the removal of sludge that has settled there for the purpose of maintaining the water body is not considered to be deepened and there is no longer any need to apply for more space for such action. In the future, it will be enough to ask for registration from the Environmental Board. Here too, the administrative burden is decreasing. The law also introduces a number of technical and terminological changes to the provisions relating, for example, to waste water treatment, to the water management plan, to the marine strategy, to the flood [risk] mitigation plan and to testing laboratories. These are the technical changes that we are going to make. It's a brief overview of the entire Water Act. Thank you and thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U706	Today's 13th item is the first reading of Bill 372 of the Law on Conformity of Products, initiated by the Government of the Republic, and related amendments to other laws. The rapporteur is Mr Andres Sutt, Minister for Entrepreneurship and Information Technology. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U707	[[Remote connection]] Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! I present today a draft law which will make it easier to withdraw dangerous products from the market, particularly in the case of e-commerce. This is necessary in order to achieve a situation where all businesses can operate under the same rules and consumers' confidence in the market is growing. The COVID crisis has accelerated the growth of e-commerce and the number of online shopping transactions has increased significantly. Thus, there is also an increased risk that end-users may be reached by products that do not meet their expectations and which may later also be a burden on our waste management system. From 16 July, the so-called market surveillance regulation directly applicable to the European Union will enter into force, which also provides for clarification in our legal order. We have tried to build on the principle that the obligations imposed on economic operators and the rights conferred on market surveillance authorities are to be achieved in proportion to the desired objective. Today, it is difficult for market surveillance authorities to identify economic operators responsible for imported but non-compliant products. That is why we are imposing an obligation to designate a contact person in the European Union, specifically for products ordered from third countries, who can be contacted if problems arise. Even if a contact person has been identified, it may not respond to a call by market surveillance authorities and may continue to sell a dangerous product. Therefore, in the absence of a serious threat and other possibilities, we give market surveillance authorities the right to request the removal of content from a web interface such as a website or application manager or a warning to dry out. We also give market surveillance authorities the right to request the necessary and minimum amount of data from the electronic communications undertaking to identify the owner of the website. In addition, we want to allow the control official, for example, to use a fictitious name at the e-shop or platform when making the control transaction in order to avoid a situation in which an undertaking issues a different product to the official. A control transaction is only permitted as a special measure in exceptional circumstances where supervision is not otherwise possible or significantly difficult. In the interests of equal treatment of economic operators, we will also harmonise some of the fines imposed by law. The law enters into force in general and the draft has been coordinated with economic operators through professional associations. Dear members of the Riigikogu, I ask you to support the bill! Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U714	Today's 14th item is the first reading of the eIdentification and Trust Services Act initiated by the Government of the Republic, the Identity Documents Act and the Amendment Act 376. The rapporteur is Mr Andres Sutt, Minister for Entrepreneurship and Information Technology. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U715	[[Remote connection]] Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! Today I present to you as a second draft draft of the Law on e-Identification and Trust Services for e-transactions initiated by the Government of the Republic, the Act on Identity Documents and the Amendment of the Law on State Fees. This is a draft, which we are probably all familiar with as an incentive to draw up. This is the security weakness of the ID card discovered in 2017, after which both MKM and the Ministry of the Interior began to thoroughly analyse the legal space in the field of electronic identity, i.e. eID, in order to find places where both the everyday efficiency of the institutions and the crisis management capacity can be improved. As a result of the work carried out, it became apparent that the eID area needs to be modernised so as to make it clearer and to allow more efficient division of labour for the institutions. This is where the first objective of the draft is raised: to resolve the specific shortcomings identified by the analysis that followed the resolution of the security weakness. For example, the draft will move the contact point for cooperation between Member States, arising from the European Union eID Regulation or eIDAS, from the Police and Border Guard Board, to the National Information System Authority, where the vast majority of specialists with the technical competence needed to carry out this task are concentrated. Resolving security weaknesses also showed that taking up these tasks with the right competences could help to avoid excessive time when problems arise. At the same time, continuous process review, competence development and risk mitigation are also a inherent part of smart risk management. Another important objective of the draft is to step towards the private sector as a country in the eID area. In other words, the draft establishes a clear and simple procedure for assessing the level of reliability of eID systems set up by private operators. Today, we are in a situation where private sector solutions have emerged alongside public eID tools, such as ID cards or mobile IDs, whose user community is at least equivalent to public funds, which in turn means that public e-services providers, or public authorities, are very interested in having access to public services with private eID tools. However, the EU eID Regulation, also known as eIDAS, obliges public authorities to assess the level of reliability of all eID tools they use to enable access to their services. The high level of reliability of national eID tools is known to the authorities, in particular because the process of setting up and managing them has been under state control since planning and procurement. In addition, the level of these instruments has been validated by other EU Member States. However, as regards private eID tools, the level of credibility assessment has not been regulated in any way so far, and therefore, in the light of their knowledge and in consultation with the RIA, the authorities have so far resolved these issues on an ongoing basis. Understandably, this approach is unsustainable, which is why the draft provides for a simple and central solution to be created to assess the credibility of private eID funds instead of the existing fragmentation. From a market position, the central national assessment may also be useful for companies providing eIDs. This is a sign of credibility, which could potentially have an impact on sales in foreign markets as well. According to the draft, the evaluation of private eID tools will be carried out by the State Information System Authority, or RIA, and all institutions will be able to rely on the credibility assessment provided by the RIA in the future. Inevitably, this task increases the workload of the RIA and other experts involved in the evaluation, which is why the draft stipulates that the evaluation is a state-feesed activity. The purpose of the State fee is to cover at least part of the costs of the assessment. Three. The EU eID regulation, which has already been mentioned, also provides that the e-services provided by our public authorities must also enable citizens of other Member States of the European Union to be logged in, using the same secure eID tools as our ID card or mobile ID. Most of the performance of this obligation is good in Estonia, but there are also individual shortcomings. This requirement must be systematically monitored in order to detect problems in a timely manner and be resolved smoothly. The draft therefore entrusts the RIA with the task of acting as a supervisory authority to monitor compliance with the obligation under European Union law in Estonia. This must in future ensure that there are no undue delays in complying with the recognition obligation. In addition to the above, the draft contains minor amendments which also update the legal space concerning identity documents and thus promote internal security. For example, the obligation under European Union law to transfer people's fingerprint data to the ID-card chip, like the passport, will be taken over. The draft also harmonises the validity of diplomatic passports with that of citizens' passports. Perhaps in the future diplomatic passports will also be valid for 10 years instead of the current five years. This will result in a reduction in the cost of issuing diplomatic passports. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! In conclusion, this is a draft which will make the work of public authorities more efficient and allow for more transparent cooperation with the private sector. I count on your support and thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U716	Thank you very much. No questions for the waitress. Thank you to the Minister. I call Jüri Jaanson, representative of the Economic Commission, as rapporteur.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-05-19_U717	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, This is a very important draft for the development of the digital state. This draft was discussed by the Commission on 10 May. a. The Commission took part in its full membership and was invited by Andres Sutt, Minister of Enterprise and Information Technology, with representatives of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications. The minister introduced the draft, as he did now, and was quite thorough. And then the members of the Commission also asked some questions. It was examined, for example, that since nine more jobs need to be made at the National Information System Office, there was a question about the cost of salary and how the resources would be found for it. The Minister replied that a long-term solution needed to be found in the basic fund, but certainly no nine jobs needed to be created at the same time. The officials commented that it is now known from the country's budget strategy that funding for these jobs is not available and that negotiations are taking place, how and what positions can be put together and what alternative solutions are available. What is important here, however, is that the draft itself does not create the need for job creation, as this need existed before. The draft was drawn up before the national budget strategy was put together and [therefore] it was examined whether the request for funding had been granted. The Minister commented that it is possible to find these people internally, but the country's budgetary strategy is not about specific amounts, but about a general view. At the same time, the Minister believes that a strong economy will ensure better budgeting and that a number of additional needs can certainly be addressed in the next country's budget strategy. According to the Minister, the crisis has been used in a good way and its conclusions have been drawn from it. It was also examined whether the level of reliability of the electronic identification system should be equivalent to the level of reliability indicated by the electronic identification service provider and what happens if, for example, the level is not equivalent but the level of service provider is better. This was answered by the officials of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications that if an entrepreneur submits an application to the RIA, or the State Information System Authority, he would indicate the level to which the instrument he was offering corresponds. The Agency shall verify whether the level is indeed equivalent. Where an undertaking assesses itself at a higher level than the Information Systems Agency, they shall refrain from taking a decision. The same applies to the contrary [in circumstances]. This means that the undertaking must know exactly what its level of credibility is and describe it. Then the decisions were made. It was decided to put the draft on the plenary agenda today, 19 May, and it was also proposed to close the first reading. Both decisions were consensual. Thank you.

Our science is good, but we do not use it enough to meet society's development needs, to solve problems and to achieve economic goals. Secondly, there is a lack of breakthrough innovation in our companies. The research and technology intensity of Estonian companies, the ability to create new business models, knowledge and technologies to adapt and adopt and to create high value added products and services is small or, in any case, smaller than they should be. A system to support the growth of business innovation capacity therefore needs to be strengthened. And thirdly, we don't have people who can or would like to do research and development. There are too few scientists and engineers and there are few outside the academy in other sectors and areas. Estonia is in a modest position in the OECD countries in terms of the number of researchers per 1000 employees, especially in the private sector. Fourthly, cooperation between R &amp; D, innovation and business actors is weak. Research institutions, companies, representative organisations, ministries, implementing bodies and other actors often act in their own right and these activities are fragmented. They do not have sufficiently clearly defined common goals, and this is certainly the reason why there is little motivation and capacity to work together. In view of these challenges, a sectoral development plan has been developed for the next 15 years. I think it's clear to everyone that we need change. We need R &amp; D, innovation and entrepreneurship to meet the needs of society's development, not only serve the interests of our sector or sector, but also society in general. We need Estonian entrepreneurship to become more science and technology-intensive. This would increase the added value and increase the productivity of the economy. We need science and researchers to grow in influence, so that researchers are good and recognised partners, both for businesses and for the public sector, in addressing problems and developing solutions. Excellence in science is a prerequisite for these changes, and we will certainly maintain and develop this high level of science. When we talk about the targets that have been set, in order for these changes to be possible, a common objective has been formulated to ensure the development of the sector. The aim of the development plan is to increase the welfare and economic productivity of Estonian society in conjunction with Estonian research and development, innovation and entrepreneurship, offering competitive and sustainable solutions to the development needs of Estonia as well as the wider world. I would like to stress that in order to achieve the objective and ambition of all of us in terms of prosperity, our productivity levels should rise to 110% of the European Union average. This means significantly greater contribution to knowledge-based and innovative solutions. We need to increase the research intensity of Estonian society, be an example for entrepreneurs as a state, keeping the level of funding for R &amp; D at least 1% of GDP. We want this to lead to an increase in the research-intensive investment of companies, because private enterprise also plays a very important role in ensuring that we achieve this 110% level. We also consider it important that our future activities and investments contribute both to solving Estonia's development needs and to making better use of the activities and strengths of Estonian entrepreneurship and science in creating breakthrough products and services with high business potential. It is also important for us to move in the same direction as the European Union's strategic goals, taking into account the European Union's green agreement, the objectives of the digital revolution, the different opportunities for cooperation between value chains, as well as between supply chains and research activities. All of this must further broaden the opportunities of Estonian researchers and entrepreneurs, especially in terms of exports. How do we meet this common goal? The activities of the development plan focus on three strands, the foundation of which is the overall development of the research system and the business environment. We need to ensure that we have a high level, effective and diverse research, strong research institutions, an active research community and its successor, a good level of research infrastructure and opportunities for international cooperation. We also need to ensure that we have a supportive business environment that promotes entrepreneurship, provides competitive conditions for research-intensive entrepreneurship and growth in the world, supports the creation and export of products and services with higher added value and research-intensive investment. And that's all over Estonia. We make Estonia the best business environment in the world, which attracts research-intensive investments and talent! Thirdly, a strong bridge between the two is essential to enable the circulation of knowledge and ideas between the research and business system and, as a result, the creation and use of new innovative solutions in society. There is a need to increase incentives and skills for cooperation between researchers, businesses and the public sector, as it is only in this way that solutions can be found to the challenges, be they more closely linked to the activities of businesses or to the wider challenges facing society. The development plan also identifies five priority areas which are key to achieving the objectives of the development plan, in particular to facilitate knowledge transfer, cooperation between public enterprises and research institutions, linking different sectors of the economy and research areas. It is important to contribute to the creation and introduction of digital solutions in all areas of business and life, so that we develop and use more innovative health technologies and services, so that we pay more attention to the enhancement of local resources, so that we develop and use smart and sustainable energy solutions so that Estonian society, language and cultural space develop and become viable. The key activities of the development plan are important, and they are a long line for this, but I would like to highlight the most important here. Firstly, in order to develop the research system, we will ensure the necessary funding and funding of the core instruments in such a way as to maintain a high level of science and sectoral diversity and to support the strategic objectives of research institutions. We support the implementation of a flexible, site-specific, academic career model that takes into account different aspects of research. We will continue the reform of doctoral studies so that doctoral students become junior researchers, guaranteeing them social guarantees and an appropriate income. We will continue to support excellence and centres of excellence. We support the development of research infrastructure services, the opening up of businesses, as well as public authorities, and provide researchers with opportunities for international cooperation. The business side is also important. With this development plan, we also ensure the necessary innovation services, support system and access to networks for companies in all areas of Estonia, reducing the administrative burden on companies in providing public services, and launching an innovation agency. The forthcoming merger between KredEx and EAS is certainly an important element in building organisational capacity that will help to achieve the R&D&I objectives. We encourage the introduction of new technologies in businesses, including the introduction of artificial intelligence and robotics, access to international technology centres, the transition to a low carbon and circular economy. We support the development of a knowledge-intensive start-up ecosystem. We provide companies with opportunities for international cooperation and encourage exports and research-intensive foreign investments. For the circulation of knowledge and for more effective cooperation between companies and research institutions, we support applied research and experimental development. I think this is one of the key points for achieving and increasing innovation intensity throughout the economy. We offer research institutions and businesses opportunities to develop knowledge transfer skills, including through awareness raising and school activities. We will create better opportunities for researchers to move between businesses and research institutions, thereby also supporting a doctorate in entrepreneurship. We also carry out thematic research programmes based on the interests of companies. Honourable Riigikogu! You will be faced with the R &amp; D, innovation and business development plan, which will formulate the development goals of the sector and set the lines of action for the next 15 years. The emphasis of the development plan has certainly changed compared to the previous one. The focus of the Action Plan is on creating a strong bridge between science and entrepreneurship, but also more broadly between society, so that the knowledge it creates is used to develop and implement innovative solutions. More important than before is the influence of science and researchers and the use of research results to meet Estonia's development needs, while maintaining our scientific excellence. And thirdly, in supporting entrepreneurship, the focus shifts from export intensity to knowledge intensity and innovation, i.e. increasing added value. If I look back in history, in 2021 we will have just as big and as weighty options as the 1992 monetary reform or membership of the European Union in 2004. We need courage at risk and international ambition in order to achieve the objectives set. Thank you.
